Norms and main proposition

Based on the above fixed point formulation and using the a priori bounds on
h
from (
3.9
),
we want to show existence and uniqueness of
\alpha
and
bound it as in (
3.25
)-(
3.26
).
As mentioned above, we will work in terms of the norms from Definition
3.5
; in particular, we will prove a contraction for the map (
3.46
) in the ‘low norm’
\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}

and bounds in the ‘high norm’
\dot{Y}^{N}
,
N:=N_{1}+12
.

Remark 3.11
.

Directly from the definition, for all
r+2|k|\leq n
, we see that

(3.52)

\displaystyle\sum_{|k^{\prime}|\leq|k|}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{1/2}\Gamma^{k^{\prime}}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha(0)\big\|}_{H^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}\lesssim\sum_{|k^{\prime}|\leq|k|}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{1/2}\underline{\Gamma}^{k^{\prime}}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}H^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}\lesssim{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{n}},

This will be used to control some of the homogeneous boundary terms that we will encounter.

We also define (see (
2.24
))

(3.53)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|f\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{n}}:=\sum_{|r|+|k|\leq n}{\big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla^{r}_{x,z}\,f\big\|}_{L^{2}_{z}L^{2}_{x}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}.\end{split}

This is the norm that we use to measure the vorticity
W
(see (
3.7
))
which appears as a forcing term in (
3.42a
). Bounds on
W
and its time derivative in the above spaces
will be bootstrapped in Section
4
.

To obtain (
3.25
)-(
3.26
) it will suffice to show the following
main proposition:

Proposition 3.12

(Bounds for
\alpha
)
.

Let
\alpha:[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{-}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}^{3}

be defined by
\alpha(t,x,z):=\beta(t,x,z+h(t,x))

where
\beta
solves the system (
3.3
) in
\mathcal{D}_{t}
.
Assume that
h
satisfies (
3.8
)-(
3.9
) and (
3.11
)-(
3.12
),
and let
W
be given so that, for
t\in[0,T]
, and for
j=0,1

(3.54)

\displaystyle{\|\partial_{t}^{j}W(t)\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{N_{1}-10-j}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1},

(3.55)

\displaystyle{\|\partial_{t}^{j}W(t)\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{N_{1}+12-j}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{j}\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}.

Then, there exists a unique fixed point
\alpha
of the map in (
3.46
)
in the space
\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}
, which satisfies

(3.56)

\displaystyle{\|\partial_{t}^{j}\alpha(t)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10-j}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1},

(3.57)

\displaystyle{\|\partial_{t}^{j}\alpha(t)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12-j}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{0}^{j}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}.

The proof of Proposition (
3.12
) is carried out in the next subsection.
The desired conclusions will be a consequence of the following main estimates:

(3.58)

\displaystyle{\|L(\alpha)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}+{\|W\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{N_{1}-10}},

(3.59)

\displaystyle{\|L(\alpha)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}}+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}+{\|W\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{N_{1}+12}},

and

(3.60)

\displaystyle{\|\partial_{t}L(\alpha)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\big({\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}+{\|\partial_{t}\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}\big)+{\|\partial_{t}W\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{N_{1}-11}},

(3.61)

\displaystyle{\|\partial_{t}L(\alpha)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+11}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\big({\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}}+{\|\partial_{t}\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+11}}\big)+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}\big({\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}+{\|\partial_{t}\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}\big)

\displaystyle+{\|\partial_{t}W\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{N_{1}+11}}

3.4.
Proof of Proposition
3.12

3.4.1.
Bounds for the Poisson kernel

We first need some bounds on the Poisson kernel.

Lemma 3.13
.

For
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
, any
p\in(1,\infty)
, and
k=0,1,\dots
, we have

(3.62)

\displaystyle{\big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k}e^{z|\nabla|}f\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}W^{r,p}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|f\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}},\qquad 1<p<\infty,

and

(3.63)

\displaystyle{\big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k}|\nabla|^{1/2}e^{z|\nabla|}f\big\|}_{L^{2}_{z}H^{r}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|f\|}_{H^{r}}.

Moreover, for
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times\{z<0\}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
we have

(3.64)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\Big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k}|\nabla|^{1/2}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|z-s||\nabla|}\mathbf{1}_{\pm}(s-z)f(x,s)\,ds\Big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}H^{r}}\\
&+{\Big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k}|\nabla|\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|z-s||\nabla|}\mathbf{1}_{\pm}(s-z)f(x,s)\,ds\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{z}H^{r}}\\
&\lesssim\min\Big(\sum_{k^{\prime}\leq k}{\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k^{\prime}}f\|}_{L^{2}_{z}H^{r}},\,\sum_{k^{\prime}\leq k}{\big\||\nabla|^{1/3}\underline{\Gamma}^{k^{\prime}}f\big\|}_{L^{6/5}_{z}H^{r}}\Big).\end{split}

Recall that
\mathbf{1}_{\pm}(x)
is the indicator function of
\pm x>0
.

Lemma
3.13
follows from standard bounds for the Poisson kernel and commutation identities for vector fields.
The proof is given in
C.2
.
Let us make a few remarks.

Remark 3.14
.

1. Note that (
3.64
) implies the same bounds for the operators

(3.65)

\displaystyle T_{1}f:=

\displaystyle\int_{z}^{0}e^{(z-s)|\nabla|}f(x,s)\,ds,\quad T_{2}:=\int_{-\infty}^{z}e^{(s-z)|\nabla|}f(x,s)\,ds,\quad T_{3}:=\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{(z+s)|\nabla|}f(x,s)\,ds,

which are those that appear in (
3.46
);
the first two are immediate, while for the last one we just observe that

T_{3}=T_{1}e^{2z|\nabla|}+e^{2z|\nabla|}T_{2}
.

2. Also note that the estimate for the second term in (
3.64
) implies
a similar estimate with
\partial_{z}
replacing
|\nabla|
:

(3.66)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\Big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\partial_{z}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|z-s||\nabla|}\mathbf{1}_{\pm}(s-z)f(x,s)\,ds\Big\|}_{L^{2}_{z}H^{r}}\lesssim\sum_{k^{\prime}\leq k}{\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k^{\prime}}f\|}_{L^{2}_{z}H^{r}};\end{split}

this follows since we have the identities

(3.67)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}T_{1}=-\mathrm{id}+T_{1}|\nabla|,\quad\partial_{z}T_{2}=\mathrm{id}-T_{2}|\nabla|,\quad\partial_{z}T_{3}=T_{3}|\nabla|,\qquad[T_{i},|\nabla|]=0.

Using these identities we can also estimate

(3.68)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\Big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\partial_{z}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|z-s||\nabla|}\mathbf{1}_{\pm}(s-z)f(x,s)\,ds\Big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}H^{r}}\lesssim\sum_{k^{\prime}\leq k}{\||\nabla|^{1/2}\underline{\Gamma}^{k^{\prime}}f\|}_{L^{2}_{z}H^{r}}+{\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k^{\prime}}f\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}H^{r}}.\end{split}

3. Bounds for higher-order
z
-derivatives also hold true: for
\ell\geq 1
,

(3.69)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\partial_{z}^{\ell}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|z-s||\nabla|}\mathbf{1}_{\pm}(s-z)f(x,s)\,ds\Big\|_{L^{2}_{z}H^{r}}\lesssim\sum_{k^{\prime}\leq k}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}\leq\ell-1}\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{z}^{\ell_{1}}f\|_{L^{2}_{z}H^{r+\ell_{2}}};\end{split}

and

(3.70)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\partial_{z}^{\ell}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|z-s||\nabla|}\mathbf{1}_{\pm}(s-z)f(x,s)\,ds\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{z}H^{r}}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{k^{\prime}\leq k}\sum_{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}\leq\ell-1}{\big\||\nabla|^{1/2}\underline{\Gamma}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{z}^{\ell_{1}}f\big\|}_{L^{2}_{z}H^{r+\ell_{2}}}+{\big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{z}^{\ell_{1}}f\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}H^{r+\ell_{2}}};\end{split}

these follow by repeatedly applying (
3.67
) to see that for
T\in\{T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}\}

we can write
\partial_{z}^{\ell}T
as a sum of terms of the form

(3.71)

\partial_{z}^{\ell_{1}}|\nabla|^{\ell_{2}},\qquad T|\nabla|^{\ell},\qquad\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}\leq\ell-1,

and then applying (
3.68
).

4. Finally, we remark that the first norm on the right-hand side of (
3.64
)
will be enough to control all the terms on the right-hand sides of (
3.47
)-(
3.48
)
except the forcing term involving the (inverse gradient of the) vorticity
for which we need to use the second norm.

We now proceed to estimate the map
L(\alpha)
in (
3.46
)-(
3.50
)
in the spaces
\dot{Y}^{n}
defined in (
3.22
)-(
3.23
).
We first prove (
3.58
)-(
3.59
)
by estimating the quantities arising from the boundary conditions (
3.47
)-(
3.48
)
in
3.4.2
, and the nonlinear bulk terms in
3.4.3
.
In
3.4.4
we control the forcing term.
Finally, in
3.4.5
we prove (
3.60
)-(
3.61
).

3.4.2.
Estimate for the homogeneous terms

In view of the bounds (
3.62
)-(
3.63
) for
e^{z|\nabla|}
,
the fact that
\partial_{z}e^{z|\nabla|}=|\nabla|e^{z|\nabla|}
, and the definition of the space
\dot{Y}^{n}
,
we have the estimate

(3.72)

\displaystyle{\big\|e^{z|\nabla|}f\big\|}_{\dot{Y}^{n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq n\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\big\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}f\big\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}.

Using this we can bound

(3.73)

\displaystyle{\big\|e^{z|\nabla|}B_{i}\big\|}_{\dot{Y}^{n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq n\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}B_{i}\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}},\qquad B\in\{B_{1},B_{2},B_{3,a}\},

(3.74)

\displaystyle{\big\||\nabla|^{-1}e^{z|\nabla|}B_{3,b}\big\|}_{\dot{Y}^{n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq n\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{-1/2+a}B_{3,b}\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}.

To get the needed estimates for
\alpha
,
we therefore want to estimate the right-hand sides of (
3.73
)-(
3.74
) and show the following:

(3.75)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-10\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}(B_{1},B_{2},B_{3,a})\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-10\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{-1/2+a}B_{3,b}\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}},\end{split}

and

(3.76)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}+12\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}(B_{1},B_{2},B_{3,a})\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}+12\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{-1/2+a}B_{3,b}\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}\\
\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}}+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}.\end{split}

Some reductions and useful estimates
.
From the definitions (
3.44
), (
3.49
) and (
3.50
)
we see that there are many terms that need to be estimated to prove (
3.75
) and (
3.76
).
However, many of them are similar and they can all be written as linear combinations of simpler terms, as we now argue.
First, (
3.44
) and (
3.49
) are all linear combinations of terms of the form

(3.77)

\displaystyle b(\nabla h,\alpha)

\displaystyle:=c(\nabla h)\alpha_{j}(0),\quad j=1,2,3,

with
c
denoting a generic coefficient satisfying

(3.78)

\displaystyle\sum_{r+2k\leq N_{1}-1}{\|c(\nabla h)\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0},\qquad p\geq 3,

(3.79)

\displaystyle\sum_{r+k\leq N_{0}-3}{\|c(\nabla h)\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},\qquad p\geq 2.

Note that we have disregarded the Riesz transform
\nabla|\nabla|^{-1}
in front of (
3.49
)
since this plays no role in the desired
L^{2}
-based estimates.
Also note that, for all practical purposes, one may think that
c=\nabla h
.

To verify (
3.77
) with (
3.78
)-(
3.79
) we inspect (
3.44
) and see that

B_{i}
is a linear combination of terms as in (
3.77
) where the coefficients are
of the form
c(\nabla h)=(1+|\nabla h|^{2})\partial_{i}h
and
\nabla h\,c(\nabla h)
;
using the product estimate (
A.21
) and the a priori assumptions (
3.9
),
we can verify directly that (
3.78
) holds: for all
p\geq 11/5

\displaystyle\sum_{r+k\leq N_{1}-1}{\|(1+|\nabla h|^{2})\partial_{i}h\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\varepsilon_{0}^{2})\sum_{r+k\leq N_{1}-1}{\|\partial_{i}h\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}

Similarly, we can use also (
3.8
) to verify (
3.79
):

\displaystyle\sum_{r+k\leq N_{0}-3}{\|(1+|\nabla h|^{2})\partial_{i}h\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(1+\sum_{r+k\leq(N_{0}-3)/2}{\|\nabla h\|}^{2}_{Z^{r,\infty}_{k}}\Big)\sum_{r+k\leq N_{0}-3}{\|\nabla h\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\varepsilon_{0}^{2})\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},

having used (
3.9
) and
N_{1}\geq N_{0}/2
.

Again omitting the Riesz transform, the term
B_{3,b}
in (
3.50
)
is a linear combination of terms of the type:

(3.80)

\displaystyle b_{3}(\nabla h,\alpha)

\displaystyle:=c(\nabla h)\,\nabla_{j}\alpha_{k}(0),

where
c_{3}
denotes a generic coefficient satisfying

(3.81)

\displaystyle\sum_{r+k\leq N_{1}-3}{\|c_{3}(\nabla h)\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{-1+(2/p)(1+\delta)},\qquad p\geq 2,

(3.82)

\displaystyle\sum_{r+k\leq N_{0}-5}{\|c_{3}(\nabla h)\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},\qquad p\geq 2.

In fact each
c_{3}
we consider is just a component of
\nabla h

and so these bounds follow directly from (
3.8
)-(
3.9
).

In view of the above reductions, we see that in order to prove
the desired bounds (
3.75
) and (
3.76
), it suffices to show that for coefficients
c,c_{3}
satisfying the above bounds,
we have

(3.83)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-10\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}c(\nabla h)\alpha(0)\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{W^{N_{1}-10}},

(3.84)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-10\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{-1/2+a}c_{3}(\nabla h)\nabla_{x,z}\alpha(0)\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{W^{N_{1}-10}},

and

(3.85)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}+12\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}c(\nabla h)\alpha(0)\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{W^{N_{1}+12}}+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\alpha\|}_{W^{N_{1}-10}},

(3.86)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}+12\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{-1/2+a}c_{3}(\nabla h)\nabla_{x,z}\alpha(0)\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{W^{N_{1}+12}}+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\alpha\|}_{W^{N_{1}-10}}.

Before proving the above estimates, we record a simple but useful product estimate
that we are going to use repeatedly below:

(3.87)

\displaystyle{\big\||\nabla|^{1/2}(fg)\big\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\|f\|}_{W^{1,3}}{\big\||\nabla|^{1/2}g\big\|}_{L^{2}},

see Lemma
A.5
.
In what follows
g
will essentially play the role of
\alpha(0)
, and
f
will be nonlinear
expressions in
h
and its derivatives.

Proof of (
3.83
).

Distributing vector fields using also (
C.23
), and applying the estimate (
3.87
),
we can bound

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-10\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}c(\nabla h)\alpha(0)\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-3\end{subarray}}{\|c(\nabla h)\|}_{Z^{r,3}_{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-10\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}\alpha(0)\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}},

having used (
3.78
) to control the coefficient.

Proof of (
3.84
).

Due to the possibly singular factor of
|\nabla|^{-1/2}
, here we distinguish the cases
a=0
and
a=1
.
If
a=0
we first apply fractional integration followed by (
A.21
):

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-10\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{-1/2}c_{3}(\nabla h)\nabla_{x,z}\alpha(0)\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-10\end{subarray}}{\|c_{3}(\nabla h)\nabla_{x,z}\alpha(0)\|}_{Z^{r,4/3}_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-10\end{subarray}}{\|c_{3}(\nabla h)\|}_{Z^{r,4}_{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-10\end{subarray}}{\|\nabla_{x,z}\alpha(0)\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}},

having used (
3.81
) for the coefficient, and (
3.52
).
When
a=1
we estimate using (
3.87
):

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-10\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2}c_{3}(\nabla h)\nabla_{x,z}\alpha(0)\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-4\end{subarray}}{\|c_{3}(\nabla h)\|}_{Z^{r,3}_{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-10\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha(0)\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}.

Proof of (
3.85
).

Distributing vector fields we can estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{r+k\leq N_{1}+12}{\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}c(\nabla h)\alpha(0)\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|r_{1}|+|k_{1}|\leq n_{1}\\
|r_{2}|+|k_{2}|\leq n_{2}\end{subarray}}{\big\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}\big(\nabla^{r_{1}}\Gamma^{k_{1}}c(\nabla h)\,\nabla^{r_{2}}\Gamma^{k_{2}}\alpha(0)\big)\big\|}_{L^{2}}:=M_{n_{1},n_{2}},

where
n_{1}+n_{2}=N_{1}+12
, and we do not make explicit the dependence on
a=0,1
which is unimportant here.
We distinguish two cases depending which of the indexes
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
is smaller.
If
n_{1}\leq N_{1}-15
we use (
3.87
) to estimate

\displaystyle M_{n_{1},n_{2}}\lesssim\sum_{|r_{1}|+|k_{1}|\leq N_{1}-13}{\|\nabla^{r_{1}}\Gamma^{k_{1}}c(\nabla h)\|}_{L^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|r_{2}|+|k_{2}|\leq N_{1}+12\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}\nabla^{r_{2}}\Gamma^{k_{2}}\alpha(0)\|}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}}

having used (
3.81
) to estimate the coefficient.
If instead
n_{2}\leq N_{1}-15
, using again (
3.87
), followed by (
3.82
), we get

\displaystyle M_{n_{1},n_{2}}\lesssim\sum_{|r_{1}|+|k_{1}|\leq N_{1}+13}{\|\nabla^{r_{1}}\Gamma^{k_{1}}c(\nabla h)\|}_{L^{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|r_{2}|+|k_{2}|\leq N_{1}-15\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}\nabla^{r_{2}}\Gamma^{k_{2}}\alpha(0)\|}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}\cdot{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}.

These last two bounds above give (
3.85
).

Proof of (
3.86
).

Distributing vector fields we have, for
a=0,1
,

\displaystyle\sum_{r+k\leq N_{1}+12}{\big\||\nabla|^{-1/2+a}\big(c_{3}(\nabla h)\nabla_{x,z}\alpha(0)\big)\big\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|r_{1}|+|k_{1}|\leq n_{1}\\
|r_{2}|+|k_{2}|\leq n_{2}\end{subarray}}{\big\||\nabla|^{-1/2+a}\big(\nabla^{r_{1}}\Gamma^{k_{1}}c_{3}(\nabla h)\cdot\nabla^{r_{2}}\Gamma^{k_{2}}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha(0)\big)\big\|}_{L^{2}}:=M_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{a},

where
n_{1}+n_{2}=N_{1}+12
.

We look at the case
a=0
first, apply fractional integration as before and then Hölder
to bound first

\displaystyle M_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{0}\lesssim\sum_{|r_{1}|+|k_{1}|\leq n_{1}}{\|\nabla^{r_{1}}\Gamma^{k_{1}}c_{3}(\nabla h)\|}_{L^{4}}\sum_{|r_{2}|+|k_{2}|\leq n_{2}}{\|\nabla^{r_{2}}\Gamma^{k_{2}}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha(0)\|}_{L^{2}};

then, when
n_{1}\leq N_{1}-15
we use (
3.81
) and (
3.52
)
to obtain
M_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{0}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{W^{N_{1}+12}}
;
when, instead,
n_{2}\leq N_{1}-15
we use (
3.82
) to obtain

M_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{0}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}\cdot{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}
.

In the case
a=1
we can use the product estimate (
3.87
) to see that

\displaystyle M_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1}\lesssim\sum_{|r_{1}|+|k_{1}|\leq n_{1}+1}{\|\nabla^{r_{1}}\Gamma^{k_{1}}c_{3}(\nabla h)\|}_{L^{3}}\sum_{|r_{2}|+|k_{2}|\leq n_{2}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2}\nabla^{r_{2}}\Gamma^{k_{2}}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha(0)\|}_{L^{2}};

then, for
n_{1}\leq N_{1}-15
we use (
3.81
) and (
3.52
) to bound

M_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{W^{N_{1}+12}}
,
and for
n_{2}\leq N_{1}-15
we use (
3.82
) and (
3.52
) to get

M_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{1}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}\cdot{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}
.

This concludes the proof
the bounds (
3.75
) and (
3.76
).

3.4.3.
Bounds for the nonlinear bulk terms

To estimate the nonlinear expressions in the bulk
integrals on the right-hand side of (
3.47
)-(
3.48
) we proceed similarly to the above,
this time using the bounds in Lemma
3.13
and Remark
3.14
first,
and then product estimates in weighted spaces.
Let us denote

(3.88)

\displaystyle\begin{split}N_{i}^{a}(\alpha)(z)&:=\int_{-\infty}^{0}\big(e^{(z+s)|\nabla|}-e^{-|z-s||\nabla|}\mathrm{sign}(z-s)\big)E^{a}_{i}(s)\,ds,\\
N_{i}^{b}(\alpha)(z)&:=\int_{-\infty}^{0}\big(e^{(z+s)|\nabla|}-e^{-|z-s||\nabla|}\big)E^{b}_{i}(s)\,ds,\qquad i=1,2,3,\end{split}

with
E^{a}
and
E^{b}
defined in (
3.43
).
We then want to show, for
i=1,2,3
,

(3.89)

\displaystyle{\|N_{i}^{\ast}(\alpha)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}},

(3.90)

\displaystyle{\|N_{i}^{\ast}(\alpha)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}}+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}},\qquad\ast\in\{a,b\},

consistently with (
3.58
) and (
3.59
).

We start by noting that the
N_{i}^{\ast}

can be written in terms of the operators
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}
in (
3.65
):

N_{i}^{a}(\alpha)=T_{1}(E^{a})-T_{2}(E^{a})+T_{3}(E^{a}),\qquad N_{i}^{b}(\alpha)=T_{1}(E^{b})+T_{2}(E^{b})+T_{3}(E^{b}).

Then, from the definition of the
\dot{Y}^{n}
norm in (
3.22
)-(
3.23
)
the estimates (
3.64
), and (
3.69
)-(
3.70
),
for
\ast\in\{a,b\}
, we have

\displaystyle{\big\|N_{i}^{\ast}(\alpha)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{n}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|r|+|k|\leq n\\
0\leq a\leq 1\end{subarray}}{\big\|\nabla_{x,z}^{a}|\nabla|^{1/2}\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla^{r}_{x,z}N_{i}^{\ast}(\alpha)\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}+{\big\|\nabla_{x,z}^{a}\nabla_{x,z}\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla^{r}_{x,z}N_{i}^{\ast}(\alpha)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle+{\big\|\partial_{z}\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla_{x,z}^{r}N_{i}^{\ast}(\alpha)\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}

(3.91)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|r|+|k|\leq n\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\big\||\nabla|^{a}\,\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla^{r}_{x,z}E^{\ast}_{i}\big\|}_{L^{2}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}+\sum_{|r|+|k|\leq n}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{1/2}\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla^{r}_{x,z}E^{\ast}_{i}\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}.

Therefore, in view of the definitions (
3.43
),
and the commutation identity (
C.23
) to handle the Riesz transform in front of
E^{a}
,
for (
3.89
)-(
3.90
) it suffices to prove the following bounds

(3.92)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|r|+|k|\leq N_{1}-10\\
|\ell|\leq 1\end{subarray}}{\big\||\nabla|^{\ell}\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla_{x,z}^{r}c(\nabla h)\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big\|}_{L^{2}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}},

(3.93)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|r|+|k|\leq N_{1}+12\\
|\ell|\leq 1\end{subarray}}{\big\||\nabla|^{\ell}\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla_{x,z}^{r}c(\nabla h)\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big\|}_{L^{2}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}}+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}},

and

(3.94)

\displaystyle\sum_{|r|+|k|\leq N_{1}-10}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{1/2}\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla_{x,z}^{r}c(\nabla h)\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}},

(3.95)

\displaystyle\sum_{|r|+|k|\leq N_{1}+12}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{1/2}\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla_{x,z}^{r}c(\nabla h)\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}}+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}},

where
c(\nabla h)
is a component of
\nabla h
or is
|\nabla h|^{2}
so that,
in particular, it satisfies

(3.96)

\displaystyle\sum_{r+k\leq N_{1}-1}{\|c(\nabla h)\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{-1+(2/p)(1+\delta)},

(3.97)

\displaystyle\sum_{r+k\leq N_{0}-3}{\|c(\nabla h)\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},\qquad p\geq 2,

in view of (
3.8
)-(
3.9
).

Proof of (
3.92
).

Distributing vector fields and using Hölder we can simply bound
the left-hand side of (
3.92
) by

\displaystyle\sum_{|r|+|k|\leq N_{1}-5}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle\Gamma^{k}\nabla^{r}c(\nabla h)\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\sum_{|r|+|k|\leq N_{1}-5}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla^{r}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big\|}_{L^{2}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-5}}

in view of (
3.96
) and the definition of the
\dot{Y}^{n}
norm.

Proof of (
3.93
).

Distributing vector fields we see that the left-hand side of (
3.93
) is bounded by
the terms

(3.98)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|r_{1}|+|k_{1}|\leq n_{1}\\
|r_{2}|+|k_{2}|\leq n_{2}\end{subarray}}{\big\|\nabla_{x}^{\ell}\big(\Gamma^{k_{1}}\nabla^{r_{1}}c(\nabla h)\cdot\underline{\Gamma}^{k_{2}}\nabla^{r_{2}}_{x,z}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}}:=B_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\ell},

with
n_{1}+n_{2}=N_{1}+12
and
\ell=0,1
.
In the case
n_{1}\leq N_{1}-15
we can bound (
3.98
) by

\displaystyle B_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\ell}\lesssim\sum_{|r_{1}|+|k_{1}|\leq N_{1}-14}{\big\|\Gamma^{k_{1}}\nabla^{r_{1}}c(\nabla h)\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\sum_{|r_{2}|+|k_{2}|\leq N_{1}+12}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle\underline{\Gamma}^{k_{2}}\nabla^{r_{2}}_{x,z}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}},

having used (
3.96
).
When instead
n_{2}\leq N_{1}-15
we can bound similarly

\displaystyle B_{n_{1},n_{2}}^{\ell}\lesssim\sum_{|r_{1}|+|k_{1}|\leq N_{1}+12}{\big\|\Gamma^{k_{1}}\nabla^{r_{1}}c(\nabla h)\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\sum_{|r_{2}|+|k_{2}|\leq N_{1}-10}{\big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k_{2}}\nabla^{r_{2}}_{x,z}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}},

having used (
3.97
).

Proof of (
3.94
).

Distributing vector fields and using (
3.87
) we can bound
the left-hand side of (
3.94
) by

\displaystyle\sum_{|r|+|k|\leq N_{1}-9}{\big\|\Gamma^{k}\nabla^{r}c(\nabla h)\big\|}_{L^{3}_{x}}\sum_{|r|+|k|\leq N_{1}-10}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{1/2}\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla^{r}_{x,z}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}

where we have used (
3.52
) for the last inequality.

Proof of (
3.95
).

Finally, we examine the left-hand side of (
3.95
),
distribute the vector fields and estimate it, using again (
3.87
),
by a linear combination of the terms

(3.99)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{1}+|k_{1}|\leq n_{1}\\
r_{2}+|k_{2}|\leq n_{2}\end{subarray}}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{1/2}\big(\Gamma^{k_{1}}\nabla^{r_{1}}c(\nabla h)\cdot\underline{\Gamma}^{k_{2}}\nabla^{r_{2}}_{x,z}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big)\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{r_{1}+|k_{1}|\leq n_{1}+1}{\big\|\Gamma^{k_{1}}\nabla^{r_{1}}c(\nabla h)\big\|}_{L^{3}_{x}}\sum_{r_{2}+|k_{2}|\leq n_{2}}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{1/2}\underline{\Gamma}^{k_{2}}\nabla^{r_{2}}_{x,z}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}:=C_{n_{1},n_{2}}.\end{split}

Then, in the case
n_{1}\leq N_{1}-15
we can bound (
3.99
) as follows:

\displaystyle C_{n_{1},n_{2}}\lesssim\sum_{|r_{1}|+|k_{1}|\leq N_{1}-14}{\big\|\Gamma^{k_{1}}\nabla^{r_{1}}c(\nabla h)\big\|}_{L^{3}_{x}}\sum_{|r_{2}|+|k_{2}|\leq N_{1}+12}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{1/2}\underline{\Gamma}^{k_{2}}\nabla^{r_{2}}_{x,z}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}},

having used (
3.96
).
When instead
n_{2}\leq N_{1}-15
we estimate

\displaystyle C_{n_{1},n_{2}}\lesssim\sum_{|r_{1}|+|k_{1}|\leq N_{1}+12}{\big\|\Gamma^{k_{1}}\nabla^{r_{1}}c(\nabla h)\big\|}_{L^{3}_{x}}\sum_{|r_{2}|+|k_{2}|\leq N_{1}-10}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{1/2}\underline{\Gamma}^{k_{2}}\nabla^{r_{2}}_{x,z}\nabla_{x,z}\alpha\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}},

having used (
3.97
) and (
3.52
).

3.4.4.
Bounds for the linear forcing terms

We now estimate the forcing term involving the vorticity on the right-hand side of (
3.47
)-(
3.48
).
These are (up to constants) given by

(3.100)

\displaystyle T_{3}F=\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{(z+s)|\nabla|}|\nabla|^{-1}F(s)\,ds,\quad\mbox{or}\quad(T_{1}+T_{2})F=\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|z-s||\nabla|}|\nabla|^{-1}F(s)\,ds,

recall
F=W
and the notation in (
3.65
).
From the identities (
3.67
) we have

(3.101)

\partial_{z}T_{3}=|\nabla|T_{3},\qquad\partial_{z}(T_{1}+T_{2})=|\nabla|(T_{1}-T_{2}).

Let
P_{>0}
and
P_{\leq 0}
denote the standard Littlewood-Paley projections (in the
x
variable)
defined according to (
2.56
)-(
2.57
), and note that they commute with the operators in (
3.100
) above.
Let us denote by
G
any of the two expressions in (
3.100
).
Using the bound in (
3.64
) by the first argument on the right-hand side,
recalling the definition (
3.53
) and (
3.22
)-(
3.23
), and
using Remark
3.14
and
(
3.101
) to handle the
z
-derivatives, we have

(3.102)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\big\|P_{>0}G\|}_{\dot{Y}^{n}}\lesssim\sum_{|r|+2|k|\leq n}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla_{x,z}^{r}P_{>0}(|\nabla|^{-1}F(s))\big\|}_{L^{2}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim{\|W\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{n}}.\end{split}

For small frequencies, we instead use the bound in (
3.64
) by the second argument on the right-hand side,
followed by fractional integration, and obtain

(3.103)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\big\|P_{\leq 0}G\|}_{\dot{Y}^{n}}&\lesssim\sum_{|r|+2|k|\leq n}{\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla^{r}_{x,z}\,|\nabla|^{1/3}P_{\leq 0}(|\nabla|^{-1}F(s))\big\|}_{L^{6/5}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{|r|+2|k|\leq n}{\big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla^{r}_{x,z}\,|\nabla|^{-2/3}W\big\|}_{L^{6/5}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{|r|+2|k|\leq n}{\big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla^{r}_{x,z}W\big\|}_{L^{6/5}_{x,z}}\lesssim{\|W\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{n}}.\end{split}

Using (
3.102
) and (
3.103
) with
n=N_{1}-10
and
n=N_{1}+12
,
we get bounds consistent with the desired inequalities (
3.58
) and (
3.59
).
The proof of (
3.58
)-(
3.59
) is thus concluded.

3.4.5.
Proof of the bounds (
3.60
)-(
3.61
)

We now prove the bounds for the time derivatives of the map
L(\alpha)
in Lemma
3.9
.
These can be obtained in the same way as the bounds (
3.58
)-(
3.59
) proved above,
using in addition the bounds on
\partial_{t}h
from (
3.11
) and (
3.12
).
We give some details for completeness.
Let us define the map
\dot{L}
through the identity

(3.104)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}L(\alpha)=L(\partial_{t}\alpha)+\dot{L}(\alpha);

Under the assumptions (
3.26
)-(
3.25
) the same exact arguments above give that

(3.105)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|L(\partial_{t}\alpha)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\partial_{t}\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}+{\|\partial_{t}W\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{N_{1}-11}},\\
{\|L(\partial_{t}\alpha)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+11}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\partial_{t}\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+11}}+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\partial_{t}\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}+{\|\partial_{t}W\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{N_{1}+11}}.\end{split}

Therefore, it suffices to prove that

(3.106)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|\dot{L}(\alpha)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}},\\
{\|\dot{L}(\alpha)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+11}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+11}}+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}.\end{split}

By definition the map
\dot{L}
is given by the right-hand side of (
3.47
) and (
3.48
)
with
F=0
, and where we replace
(E_{i}^{a},E_{i}^{b})
and
(B_{1},B_{2},B_{3,a},B_{3,b})
by
new quantities
(\dot{E}_{i}^{a},\dot{E}_{i}^{b})
and
(\dot{B}_{1},\dot{B}_{2},\dot{B}_{3,a},\dot{B}_{3,b})

defined by differentiating the coefficients that multiply
\alpha
; in other words,
for
X(\alpha)=E_{i}^{a}(\alpha),E_{i}^{b}(\alpha),B_{1}(\alpha)
and so on, we define

\displaystyle\dot{X}(\alpha)=\partial_{t}X(\alpha)-X(\partial_{t}\alpha).

More explicitly, we have

(3.107)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\dot{E}^{a}(\alpha)&:=\frac{\nabla}{|\nabla|}\cdot(\partial_{t}\nabla h)\,\partial_{z}\alpha\qquad\dot{E}^{b}(\alpha):=\big(-\partial_{t}|\nabla h|^{2}\big)\,\partial_{z}\alpha+(\partial_{t}\nabla h)\cdot\nabla\alpha,\end{split}

and

(3.108)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\dot{B}_{i}(\alpha)&:=\partial_{t}\big[(1+|\nabla h|^{2})\partial_{i}h\big]\alpha_{3}(0)-\partial_{t}\big[(1+|\nabla h|^{2})\partial_{i}h\nabla h\big]\cdot\alpha(0),\qquad i=1,2,\\
\dot{B}_{3,a}(\alpha)&:=\frac{\nabla}{|\nabla|}\cdot\partial_{t}\big(\nabla h(1+|\nabla h|^{2})^{-1/2}\big)\,\alpha_{3}(0).\end{split}

For the last boundary term, that is,
\dot{B}_{3,b}(\alpha)=\partial_{t}B_{3,b}(\alpha)-B_{3,b}(\partial_{t}\alpha)
,
we use its schematic representation from (
3.80
)
to write it as a linear combination
of terms of the form

(3.109)

\displaystyle b_{3}(\nabla h,\nabla^{2}h,\alpha)

\displaystyle:=\partial_{t}c_{3}(\nabla h)\,\nabla_{x,z}\alpha(0),

with the natural definitions of the coefficients
c_{3}
according to the formula (
3.50
).
In particular, we can verify that the following analogues of (
3.77
)-(
3.82
) hold:

-
The terms
\dot{B}_{1},\dot{B}_{2}
and
\dot{B}_{3,a}
are linear combinations of terms of the form

(3.110)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}c(\nabla h)\,\alpha_{j}(0),\quad j=1,2,3,

with

(3.111)

\displaystyle\sum_{r+k\leq N_{1}-6}{\|\partial_{t}c(\nabla h)\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0},\qquad p\geq 3,

(3.112)

\displaystyle\sum_{r+k\leq N_{0}-8}{\|\partial_{t}c(\nabla h)\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},\qquad p\geq 2;

to check the above bounds one can just
proceed as in the proofs of (
3.78
)-(
3.79
), using Lemma
A.4
,
and also the bounds for
\partial_{t}h
in (
3.11
)-(
3.12
)
besides the usual (
3.8
)-(
3.9
).

-
The term
\dot{B_{3,b}}(\alpha)
is a linear combination of terms of the form (
3.109
)
with

(3.113)

\displaystyle\sum_{r+k\leq N_{1}-8}{\|\partial_{t}c_{3}(\nabla h)\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{-3/4+(2/p)(1+\delta)},\qquad p\geq 3,

(3.114)

\displaystyle\sum_{r+k\leq N_{0}-10}{\|\partial_{t}c_{3}(\nabla h)\|}_{Z^{r,p}_{k}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},\qquad p\geq 2;

the above bounds are analogous to (
3.81
) and (
3.82
) and can be obtained in the same
way using in addition (
3.11
)-(
3.12
).
Similarly to before, for all practical purposes one may think that
c
and
c_{3}

are both just
\nabla h
.

With the formulas (
3.107
), (
3.110
) and (
3.109
),
and the estimate (
3.111
)-(
3.112
) and (
3.113
)-(
3.114
)
we can then proceed in a way completely analogous to Subsections
3.4.2
and
3.4.3
.
More precisely, as in Subsection
3.4.2
we can use (
3.62
),
and reduce matters to showing the analogues of (
3.75
)-(
3.76
) for the dotted quantities, that is,
we want to show

(3.115)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-11\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}(\dot{B}_{1},\dot{B}_{2},\dot{B}_{3,a})\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}-11\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{-1/2+a}\dot{B}_{3,b}\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}\\
\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}},\end{split}

and

(3.116)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}+11\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{1/2+a}(\dot{B}_{1},\dot{B}_{2},\dot{B}_{3,a})\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+k\leq N_{1}+11\\
a=0,1\end{subarray}}{\||\nabla|^{-1/2+a}\dot{B}_{3,b}\|}_{Z^{r}_{k}}\\
\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+11}}+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}.\end{split}

The proofs of (
3.115
) and (
3.116
)
can then be obtained in the same exact way as the proof of the bounds (
3.75
) and (
3.76
),
through analogues of (
3.83
)-(
3.86
) where the coefficients
c
and
c_{3}

are replaced by
\partial_{t}c
and
\partial_{t}c_{3}
,
and using (
3.111
)-(
3.112
) and (
3.113
)-(
3.114
); we omit the details.

Proceeding as in Subsection
3.4.3
we can let

(3.117)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\dot{N}_{i}(\alpha)(z)&:=\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{(z+s)|\nabla|}(\dot{E}^{a}_{i}(s)-\dot{E}^{b}_{i}(s))\,ds\\
&+\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-|z-s||\nabla|}(\mathrm{sign}(s-z)\dot{E}^{a}_{i}(s)-\dot{E}^{b}_{i}(s))\,ds,\qquad i=1,2,3,\end{split}

and obtain the analogues of (
3.89
) and (
3.90
)
using the estimates in Lemma
3.13
and Remark
3.14
,
the product estimate from Lemma
A.4
, (
3.87
),
and the bounds for
h
and
\partial_{t}h
in (
3.9
) and (
3.12
):

\displaystyle{\|\dot{N}_{i}(\alpha)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}},

\displaystyle{\|\dot{N}_{i}(\alpha)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+11}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+11}}+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\alpha\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}.

These are consistent with the desired (
3.106
).
and conclude the proof of (
3.60
)-(
3.61
).

3.4.6.
Conclusion

Finally, we show how the main bounds (
3.58
)-(
3.59
)
and (
3.60
)-(
3.61
) imply Proposition
3.12
.
Consider the sequence

\alpha^{(0)}=0,\qquad\alpha^{(m+1)}=L(\alpha^{(m)}),\qquad m\geq 0.

so that, under the assumptions (
3.54
)-(
3.55
),
the bounds (
3.58
)–(
3.59
) read

(3.118)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|\alpha^{(m+1)}\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha^{(m)}\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}+\varepsilon_{1}\\
{\|\alpha^{(m+1)}\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\alpha^{(m)}\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}}+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}{\|\alpha^{(m)}\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}+\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},\end{split}

and (
3.60
)–(
3.61
) read

(3.119)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|\partial_{t}\alpha^{(m+1)}\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\big({\|\alpha^{(m)}\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}+{\|\partial_{t}\alpha^{(m)}\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}\big)+\varepsilon_{1}\\
{\|\partial_{t}\alpha^{(m+1)}\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+11}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}{\|\partial_{t}\alpha^{(m)}\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+11}}+\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}\big({\|\alpha^{(m)}\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}+{\|\partial_{t}^{j}\alpha^{(m)}\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}\big)+\varepsilon_{0}\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}.\end{split}

From these we see that, for
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough, and all
t\leq T
,

(3.120)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|\alpha^{(m)}(t)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1},\qquad{\|\alpha^{(m)}(t)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},\\
{\|\partial_{t}\alpha^{(m)}(t)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1},\qquad{\|\partial_{t}\alpha^{(m)}(t)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+11}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},\end{split}

Moreover, since
L
is linear in
\alpha
, we also have that, for
j=0,1

(3.121)

\displaystyle{\big\|\partial_{t}^{j}\big(L(\alpha_{1})-L(\alpha_{2})\big)\big\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10-j}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\big({\|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10}}+{\|\partial_{t}(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2})\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11}}\big),

so that
L
is a contraction in a ball of radius
C\varepsilon_{1}
, with some absolute constant
C
,
in the space
C^{0}([0,T],\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10})\cap C^{1}([0,T],\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-11})
.
Let us denote by
\alpha
the unique fixed point of
L
in this space; we have for
j=0,1
,

(3.122)

\displaystyle{\|\partial_{t}^{j}\alpha(t)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}-10-j}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}

for all
t\leq T
.
In addition, from (
3.120
), we have
{\|\partial_{t}^{j}\alpha^{(m)}(t)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12-j}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}\varepsilon_{0}^{j}

and therefore, up to passing to a sub-sequence, we get that
\partial_{t}^{j}\alpha^{(m)}(t)
converges
weak-
\ast
in
\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12-j}
to a limit
\alpha_{j}^{\prime}(t)
,
j=0,1
.
Then we have
\partial_{t}\alpha(t)=\alpha_{j}^{\prime}(t)
for all
t\leq T
,
and by lower semi-continuity

(3.123)

\displaystyle{\|\alpha(t)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+12}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},\qquad{\|\partial_{t}\alpha(t)\|}_{\dot{Y}^{N_{1}+11}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}.

With (
3.122
) and (
3.123
) we conclude the proof of Proposition
3.12

\hfill\Box

4.
Estimates for the vorticity

In this section we bootstrap weighted bounds for the vorticity in the three dimensional (flat) domain
proving the main Proposition
2.15
.
For the convenience of the reader, and ease of reference, we restate this result below as Proposition
4.1
.
In particular this will prove the validity of the assumptions (
3.54
)-(
3.55
)
used in Proposition
3.12
to obtain (
3.56
)-(
3.57
),
which in turn give us bounds on the vector potential
V_{\omega}

and its restriction to the boundary
\widetilde{v_{\omega}}
(see Lemma
3.7
and the conclusions of Proposition
2.14
).

Let us recall here some of our notation:
with
v
the velocity field,
\omega=\curl v
, we let, for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},z\leq 0

(4.1)

\displaystyle V(t,x,z):=v(t,x,z+h(t,x)),\qquad W(t,x,z):=\omega(t,x,z+h(t,x)),

and will generally use capital letters for quantities defined in the transformed domain
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\{z\leq 0\}
;
accordingly, given the Hodge decomposition (
2.1
)
we let

(4.2)

\displaystyle\Psi(t,x,z):=\psi(t,x,z+h(t,x)),\qquad V_{\omega}(t,x,z):=v_{\omega}(t,x,z+h(t,x)).

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume that
h
satisfies (
3.8
)-(
3.9
) and (
3.11
)-(
3.12
),
and let
W
be as defined in (
4.1
).
Let
\mathcal{X}^{n}
be the space defined in (
2.24
), which for convenience we recall here:

(4.3)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\|f\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{n}}:=\sum_{|r|+2|k|\leq n}{\big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla^{r}_{x,z}\,f\big\|}_{L^{2}_{z}L^{2}_{x}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}.\end{split}

Assume that, for all
t\in[0,T]
for some
T\leq T_{\varepsilon_{1}}
, and for
j=0,1

(4.4)

\displaystyle{\|\partial_{t}^{j}W(t)\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{N_{1}-10-j}}\leq 2C\varepsilon_{1},

(4.5)

\displaystyle{\|\partial_{t}^{j}W(t)\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{N_{1}+12-j}}\leq 2C\varepsilon_{0}^{j}\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},

for some absolute constant
C>0
large enough, and where
\delta
satisfies (
2.9
).
Then, for all
t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon_{1}}]
, we have the improved bounds

(4.6)

\displaystyle{\|\partial_{t}^{j}W(t)\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{N_{1}-10-j}}\leq C\varepsilon_{1},

(4.7)

\displaystyle{\|\partial_{t}^{j}W(t)\|}_{\mathcal{X}^{N_{1}+12-j}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}^{j}\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}.

Notice that the a priori assumptions (
4.4
) and Sobolev’s embedding in
x
and
z
, imply

(4.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{|r|+|k|\leq N_{1}-15}{\big\|\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla^{r}_{x,z}\partial_{t}^{j}W(t)\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}\leq 2C\varepsilon_{1}.\end{split}

The proof of Proposition
4.1
will follow from estimates on the transport equation
satisfied by the vorticity in the transformed flat domain. Several difficulties arise in
trying to control this flow, in particular the fact that the transport velocity is not integrable in time.
We will explain in more detail below how to resolve this by a normal form type argument in the full three dimensional
(transformed) fluid domain.

4.1.
Vorticity equation and transport velocity

As a first step we write the transport of vorticity equation in the transformed flat domain.
We think of
\omega
as a vector field letting
\omega^{i}=\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{j}v_{k}
and from the vorticity equation

(\partial_{t}+v^{\ell}\partial_{\ell})\omega^{i}=\omega^{\ell}\partial_{\ell}v^{i}

and (
4.1
) we get

(4.9)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}W^{i}-\partial_{t}h\partial_{z}W^{i}+V^{\ell}\partial_{\ell}W^{i}-(V^{\ell}\partial_{\ell}h)\partial_{z}W^{i}=W^{\ell}(\partial_{\ell}-\partial_{\ell}h\partial_{z})V^{i}.

Above, and in what follows, we adopt the natural convention that
\partial_{3}h=0
.
We then write (
4.9
) as

(4.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\bf D}_{t}W=W\cdot\nabla V-W^{\ell}\partial_{\ell}h\partial_{z}V^{i}\\
&{\bf D}_{t}:=\partial_{t}+U\cdot\nabla,\qquad U:=V-(\partial_{t}h+V^{\ell}\partial_{\ell}h)e_{z}\end{split}

where
\nabla=\nabla_{x,z}
and
e_{z}=(0,0,1)
.

Next, from (
4.2
) we write, for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},z\leq 0
,

(4.11)

\displaystyle V^{i}=\partial^{i}\Psi-\partial^{i}h\partial_{z}\Psi+V^{i}_{\omega}

and then rewrite (
4.10
) as follows:

(4.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\bf D}_{t}W=W\cdot\nabla X+F,\qquad X&:=V_{\omega}+\nabla\Psi,\\
F^{i}&:=-W^{\ell}\partial_{\ell}h\partial_{z}V^{i}-W\cdot\nabla(\partial^{i}h\partial_{z}\Psi).\end{split}

The system (
4.12
) is a transport equation for
W
with a quadratic stretching term
W\cdot\nabla X

and an additional nonlinearity
F
that contains only cubic terms. Note that
W
is transported by the vector field
U
which has some components
that are not integrable in time, e.g. because of the presence of the ‘dispersive’ components

\nabla\Psi
and
\partial_{t}h
;
at the same time, some of the quadratic terms are also weakly decaying because of the presence of

\nabla\Psi
in
X
. In particular we cannot close a bootstrap argument for norms of
W
using (
4.12
) directly.
Instead, we will need to use the fact that
U
and
X
have additional structure.
This is the content of the next lemma.

Lemma 4.2
.

The vector fields
U
and
X
defined respectively in (
4.10
) and (
4.12
)
can be written as

(4.13)

\displaystyle U=\partial_{t}(A-h\,e_{z})+V_{\omega}-V_{\omega}\cdot\nabla h\,e_{z}+R,

(4.14)

\displaystyle X=\partial_{t}A+V_{\omega}+R_{2},\qquad\mbox{with}\quad A:=\nabla_{x,z}|\nabla|^{-1}e^{z|\nabla|}h,

where
R
is given by

(4.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&R^{i}=R_{1}^{i}+R_{2}^{i},\\
&R_{1}^{i}:=-\partial^{i}h\partial_{z}\Psi-(V^{\ell}-V_{\omega}^{\ell})\partial_{\ell}h\,e_{z}^{i},\\
&R_{2}^{i}:=\partial_{i}e^{z|\nabla|}|\nabla|^{-1}\big(|\nabla|\varphi-G(h)\varphi\big)+\partial_{i}(\Psi-e^{z|\nabla|}\varphi),\qquad i=1,2,3.\end{split}

Recall that
\varphi(t,x)=\Psi(t,x,h(x))
.

More precise bounds on
U
and
X
are postponed for the moment (see, for example, Lemma
4.9
).
The main point of the above lemma is that
U
(and
X
) can be written as a perfect time derivative
of one of the dispersive variables, plus terms that involve
V_{\omega}
and other
terms that will be proven to have good time-integrability properties.
In this whole section we will distinguish and treat differently,
the ‘dispersive variables’, e.g.
h
and
\nabla\Psi
, which mainly play the role of coefficients,
and the rotational variables, e.g.
V_{\omega}
and
W
.

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

From (
4.10
) and (
4.11
) we write

(4.16)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&U^{i}=\partial^{i}\Psi-\partial_{t}h\,e_{z}^{i}+V^{i}_{\omega}-V_{\omega}^{\ell}\partial_{\ell}h\,e_{z}^{i}+R_{1}^{i},\\
&R_{1}^{i}:=-\partial^{i}h\partial_{z}\Psi-(V^{\ell}-V_{\omega}^{\ell})\partial_{\ell}h\,e_{z}^{i}.\end{split}

We recall that
\psi=\psi(t,x,y)
is the harmonic extension of
\varphi(t,x):=\psi(t,x,h(x))

in the original domain, and that
\partial_{t}h=G(h)\varphi=|\nabla|\varphi+\mbox{quadratic terms}
,
and write, for
z\leq 0
,

(4.17)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Psi&=e^{z|\nabla|}\varphi+(\Psi-e^{z|\nabla|}\varphi)\\
&=e^{z|\nabla|}\partial_{t}|\nabla|^{-1}h+e^{z|\nabla|}|\nabla|^{-1}\big(|\nabla|\varphi-G(h)\varphi\big)+(\Psi-e^{z|\nabla|}\varphi).\end{split}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\begin{split}U^{i}=\partial_{t}\big(\partial^{i}e^{z|\nabla|}|\nabla|^{-1}h-h\,e_{z}^{i}\big)+\partial^{i}\big[e^{z|\nabla|}|\nabla|^{-1}\big(|\nabla|\varphi-G(h)\varphi\big)+(\Psi-e^{z|\nabla|}\varphi)\big]\\
+V^{i}_{\omega}-V_{\omega}^{\ell}\partial_{\ell}h\,e_{z}^{i}+R_{1}^{i},\end{split}

which is the desired conclusion (
4.13
)-(
4.15
).

The identity (
4.14
) is directly obtained from
X=\nabla\Psi+V_{\omega}

and the above formula (
4.17
) for
\Psi
.
∎

4.2.
Vector fields and function classes

Our next task is to apply vector fields to the equation (
4.12
).
To deal with this and handle various identities
and manipulations involving vector fields, products, commutators etc. we introduce convenient shorthand notation below.
We then also define useful classes of functions
satisfying linear and quadratic bounds consistent with our energy and dispersive estimates.

4.2.1.
Notation for vector fields

For
\underline{\Gamma}:=(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}},\partial_{z},\Omega,\underline{S})

and for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{+}^{5}
, let

\displaystyle\underline{\Gamma}^{\alpha}:=\partial_{x_{1}}^{\alpha_{1}}\cdot\partial_{x_{2}}^{\alpha_{2}}\cdot\partial_{z}^{\alpha_{3}}\cdot\Omega^{\alpha_{4}}\cdot\underline{S}^{\alpha_{5}}.

For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{+}
, we define the sets of vector fields of order
n
, respectively, less or equal to
n
, by

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{n}=\big\{\underline{\Gamma}^{\alpha},\quad\alpha\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{+}^{5}\quad\mbox{with}\quad\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}+\alpha_{5}=n\big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\leq n}=\big\{\underline{\Gamma}^{\alpha},\quad\alpha\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{+}^{5}\quad\mbox{with}\quad\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}+\alpha_{5}\leq n\big\}.

\mathcal{V}^{0}=\{1\}
, where
1
is intended as a multiplication operator.

•

(
Action of vector fields
)
Given a function
f
we will denote by
\Gamma^{n}f
a generic element of
\mathcal{V}^{n}f
, that is,

g=\Gamma^{n}f
if there exists
V\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
such that
g=Vf
.
We further denote with
\Gamma^{\leq n}f
a generic linear combination of elements in
\mathcal{V}^{\leq n}f
, that is,

g=\Gamma^{\leq n}f
if

(4.18)

\displaystyle g=\sum_{V\in\mathcal{V}^{\leq n}}c_{V}\,Vf.

for some real constants
c_{V}
.
In particular,
\Gamma^{\leq n}f
may also denote a linear combination of element of
\mathcal{V}^{n}f
.
We have the following schematic identities:

(4.19)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{n_{1}}(\Gamma^{n_{2}}f)=\Gamma^{n_{1}+n_{2}}f,\qquad\Gamma^{n_{1}}(\Gamma^{\leq n_{2}}f),\,\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}}(\Gamma^{n_{2}}f)=\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+n_{2}}f.

Note that while the
3
d vector fields are denoted
\underline{\Gamma}
using an underline,
we are not underlining the
\Gamma^{n}
expressions for ease of notation.
This should generate no confusion since in this section we work solely in the full
3
d
(flattened) domain.
When the
z
-independent function
h
is involved, the action of
\underline{\Gamma}
is the obvious one,
and we adopt the same notation
\Gamma^{n}h
and
\Gamma^{\leq n}h
.

•

(
Products
) For any
V\in\mathcal{V}^{n}
one sees that

\displaystyle V(fg)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}V_{1}\in\mathcal{V}^{n_{1}},\,V_{2}\in\mathcal{V}^{n_{2}}\\
n_{1}+n_{2}=n\end{subarray}}c_{V_{1}V_{2}}\,V_{1}f\cdot V_{2}g

for some real constants
c_{V_{1}V_{2}}=c_{V_{1}V_{2}}(V)
determined by
V
.
We then adopt a short-hand notation for linear combinations of the form above
by omitting the constants and denoting a generic term in
\mathcal{V}^{n}(fg)
as

(4.20)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{n}(fg)=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}=n}\Gamma^{n_{1}}f\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}g.

A linear combination of terms in
\mathcal{V}^{n}(fg)
will also be denoted in the same way.
Similarly, we will write a generic linear combination of elements of
\mathcal{V}^{\leq n}(fg)
,
as

(4.21)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\leq n}(fg)=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\Gamma^{n_{1}}f\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}g.

We will also use a sum as in the right-hand side of (
4.21
)
to denote a generic linear combination (of linear combinations) of products of terms
in
\mathcal{V}^{n_{1}}
and
\mathcal{V}^{n_{2}}
for
n_{1}+n_{2}=0,\dots,n
.
Note that, by this convention, we can identify

(4.22)

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}}f\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}g=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\Gamma^{n_{1}}f\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}g.

•

(
Commutators
)
From standard commutation relations one sees that for any
V_{1}\in\mathcal{V}^{n_{1}}
and
V_{2}\in\mathcal{V}^{n_{2}}

the commutator satisfies
[V_{1},V_{2}]=V^{\prime}
where

V^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}^{n_{1}+n_{2}-1}
. Consistently with this and our short-hand notation from above, we write

(4.23)

\displaystyle[\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}},\Gamma^{\leq n_{2}}]=\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+n_{2}-1}

to express the fact that the commutation of any linear combinations of vector fields of order
at most
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
, gives a linear combination of vector fields of order less or equal to
n_{1}+n_{2}-1
.

•

(
Norms and estimates
)
Consistently with the above convention,
for generic terms
\Gamma^{n}f
and
\Gamma^{\leq n}f
we have

(4.24)

\displaystyle|\Gamma^{n}f|\leq\sup_{V\in\mathcal{V}^{n}}|Vf|,\qquad|\Gamma^{\leq n}f|\lesssim\sum_{V\in\mathcal{V}^{\leq n}}|Vf|.

In particular,

(4.25)

\displaystyle{\|\Gamma^{\leq n}f\|}_{L^{q}_{z}L^{p}_{x}}\lesssim\sum_{V\in\mathcal{V}^{\leq n}}{\|Vf\|}_{L^{q}_{z}L^{p}_{x}}\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}_{+}\\
|r|+|k|\leq n\end{subarray}}{\big\|(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}})^{r}(\Omega,S)^{k}f\big\|}_{L^{q}_{z}L^{p}_{x}}.

At the same time, we also have

(4.26)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}_{+}\\
|r|+|k|\leq n\end{subarray}}{\big\|(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}})^{r}(\Omega,\underline{S})^{k}f\big\|}_{L^{q}_{z}L^{p}_{x}}\lesssim\sup_{0\leq\ell\leq n}\sup_{V\in\mathcal{V}^{\ell}}{\|Vf\|}_{L^{q}_{z}L^{p}_{x}}.

Therefore, in order to estimate the desired
\mathcal{X}^{n}
type norms of
W
, see Proposition
4.1
,
it suffices to estimate generic terms of the form
\Gamma^{\ell}W
,
0\leq\ell\leq n

in the appropriate
L^{p}_{x,z}
spaces,
and under the a priori assumptions inferred from (
4.25
),
(
4.4
)-(
4.5
) and (
4.8
).

•

(
The two dimensional case
)
We will adopt an analogous notation for the
2
d vector fields,
with corresponding product and commutator identities,
together with corresponding norms estimates as in (
4.25
).
The distinction will always be clear from context.

4.2.2.
Classes of functions

To deal with multilinear expressions involving the vorticity and the dispersive variables,
we introduce classes of linear and quadratic functions satisfying dispersive type bounds.
We will adopt the shorthand notation from Subsection
4.2.1
.
Also, recall the notation
x+
for a real number
x
(see Subsection
2.6
) and that
3p_{0}<\delta
.

Definition 4.3

(
\mathcal{O}_{i}
classes)
.

We say that a function
F=F(x,z)
defined on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{-}

is of class
\mathcal{O}_{1}
, and write
F\in\mathcal{O}_{1}
, if

(4.27a)

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n}F\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{-1+},

\displaystyle\qquad n\leq N_{1}-10,

(4.27b)

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n}F\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{3p_{0}},

\displaystyle\qquad n\leq N_{1}+12.

We say that
F
is of class
\mathcal{O}_{2}
, and write
F\in\mathcal{O}_{2}
, if

(4.28a)

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n}F\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{1+}\langle t\rangle^{-1-\delta},\qquad n\leq N_{1}-10,

(4.28b)

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n}F\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{1+}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta},\qquad n\leq N_{1}+12.

Remark 4.4

(About the
\mathcal{O}
classes)
.

Here are a few remarks and simple consequences of the above definitions:

(1)

The classes above are consistent with the bounds we have on high order energies and low order dispersive norms
for our irrotational variables.
Indeed, notice that a typical
\mathcal{O}_{1}
function is
h

together with a few (up to ten) of its derivatives, in view of (
3.8
).
The quantity
A
in (
4.13
) can also be easily seen to be in
\mathcal{O}_{1}
; see Lemma
4.6
below.
Also, clearly
\mathcal{O}_{2}\subset\mathcal{O}_{1}
.

(2)

If
F,G\in\mathcal{O}_{1}
, then the product
F\cdot G\in\mathcal{O}_{2}
.
This follows immediately from the definitions, distributing vector fields as in (
4.20
),
and using that
N_{1}
is large.
More precisely, we have

(4.29)

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n_{1}}F\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}G\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-2+},

\displaystyle\qquad n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N_{1}-10,

(4.30)

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n_{1}}F\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}G\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+3p_{0}+},

\displaystyle\qquad n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N_{1}+12,

which are sufficient since
3p_{0}<\delta
.

(3)

If
F\in\mathcal{O}_{1}
, and
W
satisfies the a priori estimates (
4.4
)-(
4.5
),
then the product
W\cdot F
satisfies estimates that are at least
\varepsilon_{0}
better.
More precisely

(4.31)

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}F\big\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\cdot\varepsilon_{1}\cdot\langle t\rangle^{-1+},\qquad n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N_{1}-10,

(4.32)

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}F\big\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\cdot\varepsilon_{1}\cdot\langle t\rangle^{3p_{0}},\qquad n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N_{1}+12,

which can be verified directly using Hölder’s inequality, estimating the terms
\Gamma^{n_{2}}F

always in
L^{\infty}_{x,z}
.

(4)

The main property that we will eventually use to bound the large number of cubic remainder terms in the
renormalized vorticity equation
is that the
L^{1}_{t}([0,T_{\varepsilon_{1}}])
-norm of the product of
W
with an
\mathcal{O}_{2}
function satisfies bounds
consistent with the conclusions (
4.6
)-(
4.7
).
This is the content of Lemma
4.5
below.

Before proceeding with some general lemmas about the behavior of
\mathcal{O}_{i}
functions,
let us recall here, for ease of reference, the bounds we established on the vector potential,
(see Lemma
3.7
and Definition
3.5
):
with the notation

(4.33)

\displaystyle V_{\omega,j}^{r,k}=\partial_{t}^{j}\underline{\Gamma}^{k}\nabla_{x,z}^{r}V_{\omega},\quad j=0,1,

we have

(4.34)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{|r|+2|k|\leq N_{1}+12-j}{\big\||\nabla|^{1/2}V_{\omega,j}^{r,k}(t)\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}+{\big\|\nabla_{x,z}V_{\omega,j}^{r,k}(t)\big\|}_{L^{2}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}+{\|V_{\omega,j}^{r,k}(t)\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{0}^{j}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},\end{split}

and

(4.35)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{|r|+2|k|\leq N_{1}-10-j}{\||\nabla|^{1/2}V_{\omega,j}^{r,k}(t)\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}+{\|\nabla_{x,z}V_{\omega,j}^{r,k}(t)\|}_{L^{2}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}+{\|V_{\omega,j}^{r,k}(t)\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}.\end{split}

In particular using the notation from
4.2.1
for vector fields,
and (
4.35
) with Sobolev’s embedding in
x
, we have the bounds

(4.36)

\displaystyle{\|\Gamma^{n}V_{\omega}(t)\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1},

\displaystyle\qquad n\leq N_{1}-12,

(4.37)

\displaystyle{\|\Gamma^{n}\nabla_{x,z}V_{\omega}(t)\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},

\displaystyle\qquad n\leq N_{1}+12,

for all
t\leq T_{\varepsilon_{1}}
.

Here is a useful Lemma about products of
W
with elements of
\mathcal{O}_{2}
.

Lemma 4.5

(Bounds on trilinear expression)
.

Let
W
be defined as in (
4.1
) and let
H\in\mathcal{O}_{2}
as in Definition
4.3
.
Then,

(4.38a)

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}H\big\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{0}^{1+}\langle t\rangle^{-1-\delta},\qquad n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N_{1}-10,

(4.38b)

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}H\big\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{0}^{1+}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta},\qquad n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N_{1}+12.

Similarly, if
H,K\in\mathcal{O}_{1}
, then

(4.39a)

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}H\cdot\Gamma^{n_{3}}K\big\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{0}^{1+}\langle t\rangle^{-1-\delta},\qquad n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq N_{1}-10,

(4.39b)

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}H\cdot\Gamma^{n_{3}}K\big\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{0}^{1+}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta},\qquad n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq N_{1}+12.

Proof.

The proof is an immediate verification. We give some details for the sake of completeness.
Using Hölder’s inequality we have

(4.40)

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}H\big\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{n_{1}+n_{2}=n}{\|\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}}{\|\Gamma^{n_{2}}H\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}.

Let us look at the case
n_{1}+n_{2}=n\leq N_{1}+12
first.
When
n_{1}\geq n_{2}
, we use the a priori bounds (
4.5
) and (
4.28a
) to estimate

\displaystyle{\|\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}}{\|\Gamma^{n_{2}}H\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|\Gamma^{\leq N_{1}+12}W\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}}{\|\Gamma^{\leq N_{1}-10}H\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}\cdot\varepsilon_{0}^{1+}\langle t\rangle^{-1-\delta}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\cdot\varepsilon_{0}^{1+}\langle t\rangle^{-1},

which suffices.
When instead
n_{2}\geq n_{1}
, we use the a priori bounds (
4.4
) and (
4.28b
) to estimate

\displaystyle{\|\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}}{\|\Gamma^{n_{2}}H\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|\Gamma^{\leq N_{1}-10}W\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}}{\|\Gamma^{\leq N_{1}+12}H\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\cdot\varepsilon_{0}^{1+}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\cdot\varepsilon_{0}^{1+}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta}.

Identical estimates hold with
L^{6/5}_{x,z}
replacing
L^{2}_{x,z}
.
This gives us the desired bound (
4.38b
).

To obtain (
4.38a
) we use the bounds on low norms (
4.4
) and (
4.28a
) and see that
for all
n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N_{1}-10
,

\displaystyle{\|\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\|}_{L^{2}\cap L^{6/5}}{\|\Gamma^{n_{2}}H\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\|\Gamma^{\leq N_{1}-10}W\|}_{L^{2}\cap L^{6/5}}{\|\Gamma^{\leq N_{1}-10}H\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\cdot\varepsilon_{0}^{1+}\langle t\rangle^{-1-\delta},

as claimed.

The proof of (
4.39
) is similar. It suffices to notice that terms of the form

\Gamma^{n_{2}}\mathcal{O}_{1}\cdot\Gamma^{n_{3}}\mathcal{O}_{1}
satisfy better bounds than

\Gamma^{\leq n_{2}+n_{3}}\mathcal{O}_{2}
, see Remark
4.4
(
2
).
∎

We conclude this section with a list of functions that are of class
\mathcal{O}_{1}
and
\mathcal{O}_{2}
.

Lemma 4.6

(Functions of class
\mathcal{O}_{1}
)
.

With the classes
\mathcal{O}_{1}
defined as in Definition
4.3
,
and under our a priori assumptions, we have

(4.41)

\displaystyle h,\,\,\partial_{t}h,\,\,A,\,\,\partial_{t}A\,\,\in\mathcal{O}_{1},

recall the definition (
4.14
), and

(4.42)

\displaystyle V-V_{\omega},\,\,\nabla_{x,z}\Psi\,\,\in\mathcal{O}_{1},

see (
4.1
) and (
4.2
).
The same holds true for
\Gamma^{\leq 2}
applied to all the quantities in (
4.41
)-(
4.42
).

Proof.

The fact that
h\in\mathcal{O}_{1}
follows directly from the a priori assumption (
2.20
) and (
2.22
).
For
\partial_{t}h
we use instead that
\partial_{t}h=G(h)\varphi
and the bounds in (
B.37
)
to deduce bounds as in (
4.27a
)-(
4.27b
).

For
A=\nabla_{x,z}|\nabla|^{-1}e^{z|\nabla|}h
we use
Sobolev’s embedding in
x
followed by (
3.62
):
for
n\leq N_{1}+12
, and denoting
\mathcal{R}=\nabla|\nabla|^{-1}
the (vector) Riesz transform,

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n}\nabla_{x,z}|\nabla|^{-1}e^{z|\nabla|}h\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\big\|\Gamma^{n}(1,\mathcal{R})e^{z|\nabla|}h\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{z}H^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\sup_{|r|+|k|\leq n}{\|h\|}_{Z_{k}^{r+2}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{p_{0}}

which is sufficient for the bound (
4.27b
) for this term.
To verify the bound (
4.27a
) we proceed similarly: for
n\leq N_{1}-10
we have,
by the maximum principle, Sobolev’s embedding, and (
3.9
),

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n}\nabla_{x,z}|\nabla|^{-1}e^{z|\nabla|}h\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}\lesssim{\|(1,\mathcal{R})\Gamma^{n}h\|}_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sup_{|r|+|k|\leq n}{\|h\|}_{Z_{k}^{r+1,\infty-}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{-1+}.

We also see that applying
\Gamma^{\leq 2}
to any of the quantities in (
4.41
)
we still obtain
\mathcal{O}_{1}
functions.

Since
V-V_{\omega}=\nabla_{x,z}\Psi-\nabla h\,\partial_{z}\Psi
,
in view of Remark
4.4
, we see that in order to obtain (
4.42
) it suffices
to prove that
\nabla_{x,z}\Psi\in\mathcal{O}_{1}
.
This is a consequence of Lemma
B.6
.
Indeed, the property (
4.27b
) follows directly from the first bound in (
B.48
)
and Sobolev’s embedding.
The bound (
4.27a
) follows from (
B.49
)
which gives a stronger bound for
\sum_{\ell}P_{\ell}\nabla_{x,z}\Psi
with
2^{\ell}\in[\langle t\rangle^{-5},\langle t\rangle^{5}]
,
combined with the
L^{2}
bound (
B.48
) for the remaining very small and very large frequencies.
∎

Lemma 4.7

(Functions of class
\mathcal{O}_{2}
)
.

With the definitions (
4.15
), we have

(4.43)

\displaystyle R_{1},\quad\nabla_{x,z}(\Psi-e^{z|\nabla|}\varphi),\quad\nabla_{x,z}e^{z|\nabla|}|\nabla|^{-1}\big(|\nabla|\varphi-G(h)\varphi\big)\,\in\,\mathcal{O}_{2},

The same is also true for
\Gamma^{\leq 2}
applied to all of the above quantities.
In particular,

(4.44)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\leq 2}R_{2},\quad\Gamma^{\leq 2}R\,\in\,\mathcal{O}_{2}.

Proof.

Since
R_{1}^{i}=-\partial^{i}h\partial_{z}\Psi-(V^{\ell}-V^{\ell}_{\omega})\partial_{\ell}h\,e_{z}^{i}

we see that this is in the
\mathcal{O}_{2}
class in view of (
4.41
)-(
4.42
)
and (
2
) of Remark
4.4
.

The second and third term in (
4.43
) are almost the same.
For the second term we can use directly (
B.50
), respectively (
B.51
),
and Sobolev’s embedding to deduce (
4.28b
) (recall
\delta>3p_{0}
), respectively, (
4.28a
).

For the third term in (
4.43
) we use the notation from (
B.22
), that is,

G_{\geq 2}(h)\varphi:=G(h)\varphi-|\nabla|\varphi
, and invoke the bounds (
B.38
).
Using the maximum principle and Sobolev’s embedding we obtain, for
n\leq N_{1}+12
,

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n}\nabla_{x,z}|\nabla|^{-1}e^{z|\nabla|}G_{\geq 2}(h)\varphi\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{|r|+|k|\leq n}{\|(1,\mathcal{R})G_{\geq 2}(h)\varphi\|}_{Z_{k}^{r,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{|r|+|k|\leq n}{\|(1,\mathcal{R})G_{\geq 2}(h)\varphi\|}_{Z_{k}^{r+2}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+3p_{0}}.

Similarly, we can use (
3.62
) to obtain, for
n\leq N_{1}-10
,

\displaystyle{\big\|\Gamma^{n}\nabla_{x,z}|\nabla|^{-1}e^{z|\nabla|}G_{\geq 2}(h)\varphi\big\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{|r|+|k|\leq n}{\|(1,\mathcal{R})G_{\geq 2}(h)\varphi\|}_{Z_{k}^{r,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{|r|+|k|\leq n}{\|G_{\geq 2}(h)\varphi\|}_{Z_{k}^{r+1,\infty-}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-6/5}.

having used
L^{p}
interpolation between the bounds in (
B.38
).
∎

4.3.
Commutation with vector fields

We proceed to derive a transport equation for
\Gamma^{n}W
.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
{\bf D}_{t}:=\partial_{t}+U\cdot\nabla
and recall the notation in
4.2.1
.
We have:

(1)

The following basic commutation identities hold

(4.45)

\displaystyle\begin{split}[{\bf D}_{t},\partial_{x_{i}}]&=-\partial_{x_{i}}U\cdot\nabla,\quad i=1,2,3,\quad(x_{3}=z),\\
[{\bf D}_{t},\Omega]&=U_{1}\partial_{x_{2}}-U_{2}\partial_{x_{1}}-\Omega U\cdot\nabla,\\
[{\bf D}_{t},S]&=(1/2){\bf D}_{t}+(1/2)U\cdot\nabla-SU\cdot\nabla.\end{split}

(2)

For any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{+}^{5}
,
|\alpha|=1
,
there exists constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{2}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
such that

(4.46)

\displaystyle[{\bf D}_{t},\Gamma^{\alpha}]=c_{1}{\bf D}_{t}+c_{2}U\cdot\nabla+c_{2}^{\prime}U\cdot\nabla_{x}^{\perp}-\Gamma^{\alpha}U\cdot\nabla.

Since the presence of the
⟂
and of all the constants will not play any role,
we will drop them from (
4.46
) and use the notation conventions from
4.2.1
to write,
for some
c\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

(4.47)

\displaystyle[{\bf D}_{t},\Gamma^{1}]f=c{\bf D}_{t}f+(\Gamma^{\leq 1}U)\cdot\nabla f.

(3)

If
{\bf D}_{t}W=B
then, for all
n\geq 1
,

(4.48)

\displaystyle{\bf D}_{t}\Gamma^{n}W=\Gamma^{\leq n}B+\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-1}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}U\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W;

here the sum is a linear combination as per our conventions, see (
4.21
) and the paragraph following that.

(4)

If
W
is the solution of (
4.12
), then, for any integer
n
we have

(4.49)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\bf D}_{t}\Gamma^{n}W&=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-1}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}U\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W+\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla X+\Gamma^{\leq n}F.\end{split}

Proof.

The identities (
4.45
) follow from standard calculations.
Indeed, for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2}_{-}
we have

\displaystyle[{\bf D}_{t},S]

\displaystyle=(1/2)[\partial_{t},t\partial_{t}]+[U\cdot\nabla,(1/2)t\partial_{t}+x\cdot\nabla]

\displaystyle=(1/2)\partial_{t}-SU\cdot\nabla-U\cdot[S,\nabla]

\displaystyle=(1/2){\bf D}_{t}-(1/2)U\cdot\nabla-SU\cdot\nabla-U\cdot(-\nabla),

which is of the desired form.
Similarly,

[{\bf D}_{t},\Omega]=[U\cdot\nabla,x_{1}\partial_{x_{2}}-x_{2}\partial_{x_{1}}]=U_{1}\partial_{x_{2}}-U_{2}\partial_{x_{1}}-\Omega U\cdot\nabla.

The identity (
4.46
), and its shorthand version (
4.47
),
then follow directly from (
4.45
).

To prove (
4.48
) we proceed by induction.
The case
n=1
follows directly from (
4.47
).
Assuming (
4.48
) holds true for
n=1

we calculate using the inductive hypothesis and (
4.47
):

\displaystyle{\bf D}_{t}\Gamma^{n}W

\displaystyle=\Gamma^{1}{\bf D}_{t}\Gamma^{n-1}W+[{\bf D}_{t},\Gamma^{1}]\Gamma^{n-1}W

\displaystyle=\Gamma^{1}\Big(\Gamma^{\leq n-1}B+\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-2}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}U\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W\Big)

\displaystyle+{\bf D}_{t}\Gamma^{n-1}W+\Gamma^{\leq 1}U\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{n-1}W;

distributing the vector field
\Gamma
in the first line (see (
4.20
)),
applying again the inductive hypothesis to
{\bf D}_{t}\Gamma^{n-1}W
,
and commuting the
\Gamma
’s and
\nabla
in the last term, we see that the above expression is of the desired form (
4.48
).

Finally, the equation (
4.49
) follows from applying the previous identity (
4.48
)
to the equation (
4.12
).
∎

We also have the following lemma for the transporting vector field
U
.

Lemma 4.9
.

With the notation of
4.2.1
and under the assumptions of Proposition
4.1
,
for
U
and
X
as in (
4.13
) we have:

(4.50)

\displaystyle U=\mathcal{O}_{1}+V_{\omega}+V_{\omega}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{1},\qquad\nabla X=\mathcal{O}_{1}+\nabla V_{\omega},

In particular

(4.51)

\displaystyle{\|\Gamma^{n}U\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{-1+}+\varepsilon_{1},\qquad n\leq N_{1}-12.

Proof.

We see from the formulas in Lemma
4.2
,
and using Lemmas
4.6
and
4.7
, that

(4.52)

\displaystyle U=\mathcal{O}_{1}+V_{\omega}-V_{\omega}\cdot\nabla h\,e_{z}+\mathcal{O}_{2},

and (
4.50
) follows.
∎

4.4.
Renormalization of the vorticity equation

In this subsection we manipulate the equation for
W
, see (
4.49
),
using the identities from Lemma
4.2
, in order to write it in a better form
that allows to propagate the desired
\mathcal{X}^{n}
norms and prove Proposition
4.1
.
These manipulations are akin to a (partial) normal form transformation on the vorticity equation
in the full three dimensional fluid domain that effectively renormalizes the irrotational components.
The next proposition is the main result of this section.

Proposition 4.10

(Renormalized vorticity equation)
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
4.1

we have following:
for all
n\leq N_{1}+12
, there exist
corrections
G^{n}=G^{n}(t,x,z)
such that

(4.53)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathbf{D}_{t}}\big(\Gamma^{n}W-G^{n}\big)=Q_{1}^{n}+Q_{2}^{n}+C_{1}^{n}+C_{2}^{n}+F^{n}\end{split}

where the following holds:

•

The correction
G^{n}
satisfies for all
|t|\leq T

(4.54a)

\displaystyle{\|G^{n}(t)\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\varepsilon_{1},\qquad n\leq N_{1}-10,

(4.54b)

\displaystyle{\|G^{n}(t)\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{\delta},\qquad n\leq N_{1}+12.

•

Q_{1}^{n},Q_{2}^{n}
are quadratic terms (in the rotational variables only) given by

(4.55a)

\displaystyle Q_{1}^{n}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla V_{\omega},

(4.55b)

\displaystyle Q_{2}^{n}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-1}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}V_{\omega}\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W.

•

C^{n}_{1},C^{n}_{2}
are cubic terms of the form

(4.56)

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{1}^{n}&:=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq n}\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla V_{\omega}\cdot\Gamma^{n_{3}}\mathcal{O}_{1},\\
C_{2}^{n}&:=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}\leq n-1}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}V_{\omega}\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{3}}\mathcal{O}_{1};\end{split}

recall Definition
4.3
and Lemma
4.6
.

•

The remaining nonlinear terms satisfy

(4.57a)

\displaystyle{\|F^{n}\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{0}^{1+}\langle t\rangle^{-1-\delta},\qquad n\leq N_{1}-10,

(4.57b)

\displaystyle{\|F^{n}\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\varepsilon_{0}^{1+}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta},\qquad n\leq N_{1}+12.

Remark 4.11

(“Correction” and “Acceptable Remainders”)
.

Here are some remarks on Proposition
4.10
:

•

G^{n}
is a normal-form type “correction” of
\Gamma^{n}W
since its norms are
\varepsilon_{0}
smaller than those of
\Gamma^{n}W

(compare (
4.54
) and (
4.4
)-(
4.5
)) and the equation satisfied by
\Gamma^{n}W-G^{n}

has nonlinear terms that are more perturbative then the ones in (
4.49
).

•

We call a (cubic) term that satisfies (
4.57
) an “acceptable remainder”
since such a term gives a small perturbation of the transported vector field
\Gamma^{n}W-G^{n}
(hence of
\Gamma^{n}W
)
when integrated in over time
t\in[0,T_{\varepsilon_{1}}]
.
Many terms will be shown to be acceptable remainders directly using the lemmas from the previous subsection.

•

The quadratic terms on the right-hand side of (
4.53
) only depend on
W
and
V_{\omega}
.
Technically, they are not acceptable remainders in the sense specified above and so will be estimated separately in Subsection
4.5
.

Proof of Proposition
4.10
.

We start from (
4.49
) and use the structure of the vector fields
U
and
X
,
see Lemmas
4.2
and
4.9
, to eventually obtain (
4.53
).
In the course of the proof we are going to collect several remainders denoted by
F^{n}_{1},F^{n}_{2}
and similar,
that will eventually contribute to the nonlinear remainder
F^{n}
.

Step 1: Renormalized equation
.
First, for convenience of the reader, we recall (
4.49
)

(4.58)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\bf D}_{t}\Gamma^{n}W&=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-1}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}U\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W+\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla X+\Gamma^{\leq n}F.\end{split}

Using the formulas for
A
and
X
in (
4.14
), and the definition of
F
in (
4.12
),
we rewrite (
4.58
) in the following form:

(4.59a)

\displaystyle{\bf D}_{t}\Gamma^{n}W

\displaystyle=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-1}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}\partial_{t}(A-he_{z})\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W+\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\Gamma^{n_{2}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{1}}\nabla\partial_{t}A

(4.59b)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-1}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}V_{\omega}\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W+\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla V_{\omega}

(4.59c)

\displaystyle-\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-1}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}(V_{\omega}\cdot\nabla he_{z})\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W+\Gamma^{\leq n}\big(-W\cdot\nabla h\,\partial_{z}V_{\omega}\big)

(4.59d)

\displaystyle+F^{n}_{0}+F^{n}_{1}+F^{n}_{2},

where we define

(4.60)

\displaystyle F^{n}_{0}

\displaystyle:=\Gamma^{\leq n}\big(-W\cdot\nabla h\,\partial_{z}(V-V_{\omega})-W\cdot\nabla(\nabla h\partial_{z}\Psi)\big),

(4.61)

\displaystyle F^{n}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-1}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}R\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W,

(4.62)

\displaystyle F^{n}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\Gamma^{n_{1}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla R_{2}.

We analyze term by term the right-hand side of (
4.59
).
First, we see that the terms in (
4.59b
) contribute to the quadratic
terms on the right-hand side of (
4.53
) as they match exactly (
4.55
), and so they are accounted for.
Similarly, the cubic terms in (
4.59c
) are accounted for in the terms (
4.56
),
since up to two derivatives of
h
are in
\mathcal{O}_{1}
, see Lemma
4.6
.

Then, since up to two derivatives of
h
, and one derivative of
\partial_{z}\Psi
,
and the term
\partial_{z}(V-V_{\omega})
are in
\mathcal{O}_{1}
, see Lemma
4.6
,
we see that (
4.60
) is of the form

\Gamma^{\leq n}(W\cdot\mathcal{O}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{1})=\Gamma^{\leq n}(W\cdot\mathcal{O}_{2}),

see Remark
4.4
; applying Lemma
4.5
and (
4.20
)
we obtain that
F^{n}_{0}
is an acceptable remainder in that it satisfies the bounds (
4.57
).

We can easily see that the term (
4.61
) is an acceptable remainder using that
R\in\mathcal{O}_{2}
,
see Lemma
4.7
and (
4.15
), and an application of Lemma
4.5
.
Similarly, the term (
4.62
) is an acceptable remainder using that
\nabla R_{2}\in\mathcal{O}_{2}
,
see Lemma
4.7
.

We now analyze the two terms in (
4.59a
). For the first one
we use
\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}\partial_{t}=\partial_{t}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}={\mathbf{D}_{t}}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}+U\cdot\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}
to write

(4.63)

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-1}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}\partial_{t}(A-he_{z})\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-1}{\mathbf{D}_{t}}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}(A-he_{z})\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W+F^{n}_{3},

\displaystyle\qquad F^{n}_{3}:=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-1}U\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}(A-he_{z})\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W.

The first term on the right-hand side of (
4.63
) will be analyzed shortly below.
First, we verify that
F^{n}_{3}
is an acceptable remainder satisfying (
4.57
); indeed
we can write

\displaystyle F^{n}_{3}:=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-1}U\cdot\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}\mathcal{O}_{1}\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W

and observe that
U\cdot\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}\mathcal{O}_{1}\in\Gamma^{n_{1}+1}\mathcal{O}_{2}
,
since
U
satisfies (
4.51
).

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
4.59a
)
we can use again
\Gamma^{n_{1}}\nabla\partial_{t}={\mathbf{D}_{t}}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}}\nabla+U\cdot\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}}\nabla
to write

(4.64)

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\Gamma^{n_{2}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{1}}\nabla\partial_{t}A

\displaystyle=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\Gamma^{n_{2}}W\cdot{\mathbf{D}_{t}}\Gamma^{n_{1}}\nabla A+F^{n}_{4}

where

(4.65)

\displaystyle F^{n}_{4}:=U\cdot\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\Gamma^{n_{2}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{1}}\nabla A.

Since
U=V_{\omega}+\mathcal{O}_{1}+V_{\omega}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{1}
, see (
4.50
), it is not hard to verify that
F^{n}_{4}
is an acceptable remainder.
Indeed, by (
3
) in Remark
4.4
, since
\nabla A\in\mathcal{O}_{1}
,
the quadratic terms in the sum (
4.65
) are bounded by the right-hand side of (
4.32
),
respectively (
4.31
),
when
n\leq N_{1}+12
, respectively
n\leq N_{1}-10
;
since we also have that
{\|U\|}_{L^{\infty}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{-1+}+\varepsilon_{1}

in view of (
4.13
), (
4.41
) and (
4.36
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\|F^{n}_{4}\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-1+}\cdot(\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{-1+}+\varepsilon_{1}),\qquad n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N_{1}-10,

\displaystyle{\|F^{n}_{4}\|}_{L^{2}_{x,z}\cap L^{6/5}_{x,z}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{3p_{0}}\cdot(\varepsilon_{0}\langle t\rangle^{-1+}+\varepsilon_{1}),\qquad n_{1}+n_{2}\leq N_{1}+12.

These bounds are enough for (
4.57a
)-(
4.57b
) since
3p_{0}<\delta
,
\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{1}
,
and
t\leq T_{\varepsilon_{1}}=\varepsilon_{1}^{-1+\delta}
gives
\varepsilon_{1}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-1-\delta}
.

We now combine (
4.63
)-(
4.65
),
changing the index in the sums
and then pull the
{\mathbf{D}_{t}}
out to write the first two terms on the right-hand side of (
4.59a
)
as

(4.66)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n-1}\partial_{t}\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}(A-he_{z})\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}\nabla W+\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}\Gamma^{n_{2}}W\cdot\Gamma^{n_{1}}\nabla\partial_{t}A\\
&=\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}\leq n}{\mathbf{D}_{t}}\big(\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}A+\Gamma^{\leq n_{1}+1}he_{z}\big)\cdot\Gamma^{n_{2}}W+F^{n}_{3}+F^{n}_{4}\\
&=:{\bf D}_{t}G^{n}+F^{n}_{3}+F^{n}_{4}+F^{n}_{5},\end{split}

upon defining

(4.67)
